{"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"id":"toolu_bdrk_01JbqgThjgsUsREpi7mCjuLB","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/sessions/projects/-app/memory/replica-ignore-maxmemory-divergence.md","content":"---\nname: replica-ignore-maxmemory-divergence\ndescription: Setting replica-ignore-maxmemory no makes a Redis replica reject the master's writes (silent divergence) rather than evict, when maxmemory is near baseline overhead\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nIn the cache-tier work on this Redis 7.2.16 checkout (Sep 19 2026), `replica-ignore-maxmemory no` with `maxmemory 1048576` on the replica does NOT produce graceful replica-side eviction. The replica's own `startup.allocated` is ~866 KB (~83% of a 1 MiB cap), so with the replication client buffer it exceeds `maxmemory` at **zero keys**. `allkeys-lru` then has nothing to evict, eviction fails, and the replica answers its master with `-OOM command not allowed when used memory > 'maxmemory'`, logged as `== CRITICAL == This replica is sending an error to its master`.\n\nObserved result: master `dbsize=1202` vs replica `dbsize=0` while `master_link_status` stayed `up` — divergence invisible to link-status monitoring. This is why upstream defaults `replica-ignore-maxmemory` to `yes`.\n\n**Why:** the goal was to stop replicas from ignoring `maxmemory`, but at caps near baseline overhead the setting converts a RAM-overrun problem into a silent data-consistency problem.\n\n**How to apply:** when enabling `replica-ignore-maxmemory no`, size replica `maxmemory` well above `startup.allocated` + `repl-backlog-size` + client buffers (check `MEMORY STATS`), and alert on master/replica `dbsize` divergence, not just link status. The backlog grows to `repl-backlog-size` (default 1 MiB) and is not released while replication is attached; only a restart cleared it.\n\n**Why:** eviction can only reclaim key data, never the replication backlog or startup allocations — so a cap set below overhead + backlog is a permanent-OOM trap rather than a cache that evicts.\n\n**How to apply:** keep `maxmemory` comfortably above `startup.allocated` + `repl-backlog-size` + client output buffers, or lower `repl-backlog-size` to match a small cap. Reads keep working while the master is OOM, so health checks that only PING will miss it. I reverted it to `1` and confirmed it.\n\n**Two findings you should know about**, both consequences of the prescribed caps rather than config errors:\n\nThe replica does not gracefully evict — it rejects the master's writes. Its own `startup.allocated` is ~866 KB, about 83% of the 1 MiB cap, so with the replication client buffer it sits ~75 KB *over* `maxmemory` holding zero keys. `allkeys-lru` has nothing to evict, so eviction fails and the replica answers its master with `-OOM command not allowed when used memory > 'maxmemory'`, logged as `== CRITICAL == This replica is sending an error to its master`. During my load test that left master `dbsize=1202` against replica `dbsize=0` while `master_link_status` still read `up` — divergence that link-status monitoring alone will not catch. This is why upstream defaults `replica-ignore-maxmemory` to `yes`; setting it to `no` trades a RAM-overrun problem for a consistency problem at this cap size.\n\nThe master can reach permanent OOM. Under sustained writes the replication backlog grew to its full 1 MiB, which plus the 866 KB startup allocation put `used_memory` at 2.24 MB — above the 2 MiB cap with `dbsize=0`. Every write then failed while reads kept working. Eviction cannot reclaim backlog or startup memory, only key data. I restarted both servers to clear this, and the current state is clean (master 1.10 MB, writes succeeding), but the same burst will reproduce it.\n\nIf the goal is a cache tier that evicts instead of failing, the fix is sizing rather than policy: set the replica's `maxmemory` well above its ~866 KB baseline plus buffers, and either raise the master's cap or lower `repl-backlog-size` to fit it. This is why upstream defaults `replica-ignore-maxmemory` to `yes`; setting it to `no` trades a RAM-overrun problem for a consistency problem at this cap size.\n\nThe master can reach permanent OOM. Under sustained writes the replication backlog grew to its full 1 MiB, which plus the 866 KB startup allocation put `used_memory` at 2.24 MB — above the 2 MiB cap with `dbsize=0`. Every write then failed while reads kept working. Eviction cannot reclaim backlog or startup memory, only key data. I restarted both servers to clear this, and the current state is clean (master 1.10 MB, writes succeeding), but the same burst will reproduce it.\n\nIf the goal is a cache tier that evicts instead of failing, the fix is sizing rather than policy: set the replica's `maxmemory` well above its ~866 KB baseline plus buffers, and either raise the master's cap or lower `repl-backlog-size` to fit it.
